# GSQ224 source admission and bounded quality screen

The rejected minmax223 expert payload was retired only after authenticating every file and preserving all manifests and exact reconstruction sources. The existing retirement procedure was reused with a closed forty-eight-file scope and a hardlink-aware staging census; all seven existing refusal/recovery tests passed for that scope. The original installation and other payloads were unchanged.

The first full-shard Hub download failed after a read timeout and DNS error. Its internal resume attempt and terminal failure are preserved; it left no admitted payload. A read-only exact-range connectivity check then passed. A separate frozen bounded-range acquisition retained progress, allowed four sixty-four-MiB responses per batch, and verified the complete immutable LFS SHA256. Acquisition is not inference qualification.

The base repository still has one non-README payload map across its three visible revisions. The GSQ card and GGUF name that base. This supports the same-published-checkpoint lineage inference already used for other derivative research; exact calibration/producer provenance has not been independently replayed.

The quality instrument reuses the existing affine layer-streamed reference, original tokenizer contexts and full-vocabulary scoring. It first reproduces the known three-bit control exactly. The candidate replaces only gate/up projections with GSQ Q2_0, explicitly rounding scales/biases to BF16, and retains original four-bit down and all non-expert tensors. The whole source file and inspected header prefix are authenticated through one retained descriptor. No new weights or raw logits are exported. Pre-execution V1/V2 refinements and V3's changed acquisition path are preserved; none changed the fixed contexts, ten-GB physical limit or proxy advance criteria.

The complete raw receipts below determine the outcome. This is a distribution screen, not completed-task quality, native full-model speed, another-Mac result, product installation or Auto admission.
